Local existence and uniqueness of solution to the two-dimensional inhomogeneous Prandtl equations by energy method
Jincheng Gao, Lianyun Peng, Zheng-an Yao[10pt] School of Mathematics, Sun Yat-Sen University,510275, Guangzhou, P. R. China[5pt]

Abstract
In this paper, we consider the local existence and uniqueness result for the inhomogeneous Prandtl equations in dimension two by energy method. First of all, for the homogeneous case, the local-in-time well-posedness theory of unsteady Prandtl equations was obtained by [Alexandre, Wang, Xu, Yang, J. Am. Math. Soc., 28 (3), 745-784 (2015)] and [Masmoudi, Wong, Comm. Pure Appl. Math., 68 (10), 1683-1741 (2015)] independently by energy method without any transformation. However, for the inhomogeneous case, the appearance of density will create some new difficulties for us to overcome the loss of tangential derivative of horizontal velocity. Thus, our first result is to overcome the loss of tangential derivative such that one can establish the local-in-time well-posedness result for the inhomogeneous Prandtl equations by energy method. Secondly, for the homogeneous case, the local-in- x x well-posedness in higher regular space for the steady Prandtl equations was obtained by [Guo, Iyer, Comm. Math. Phys., 382 (3), 1403-447 (2021)] by energy method since they firstly found the good quantity(called ‘quotient’). With the help of this quotient, our second result is to establish the local-in- x x well-posedness in higher regular Sobolev space for the steady inhomogeneous Prandtl equations. Keywords: inhomogeneous Prandtl equations, well-posedness theory, energy method.

1
Introduction

In this paper, we are concerned with the initial-boundary value problem for the
following inhomogeneous Prandtl equations in a periodic domain
\Omega\overset{def}{=}\{(x,y)|(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\}
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\rho+u\partial_{x}\rho+v\partial_{y}\rho=0,\\
&\rho\partial_{t}u+\rho u\partial_{x}u+\rho v\partial_{y}u-\partial_{y}^{2}u+\partial_{x}p=0,\\
&\partial_{x}u+\partial_{y}v=0,\\
&(u,v)|_{y=0}=0,\underset{y\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}(\rho,u)(t,x,y)=(\rho_{e}(t,x),u_{e}(t,x)),\\
&(\rho,u)|_{t=0}=(\rho_{0},u_{0})(x,y).\end{aligned}\right.

(1.1)

where
\rho
denotes the density,
(u,v)
denotes the fluid horizontal
and vertical velocity field,
p
denotes the pressure.
The known function
(\rho_{e}(t,x),u_{e}(t,x),p(t,x))

satisfies the Bernoulli law:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\rho_{e}+u_{e}\partial_{x}\rho_{e}=0,\\
&\rho_{e}\partial_{t}u_{e}+\rho_{e}u_{e}\partial_{x}u_{e}+\partial_{x}p=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(1.2)

The Prandtl equations (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) can be derived from the inhomogeneous Navier-Stokes equations, which can be found in Appendix
E
in detail.

When the flow is steady, the system (
1.1
) is reduced to the following equations:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &u\partial_{x}\rho+v\partial_{y}\rho=0,\\
&\rho u\partial_{x}u+\rho v\partial_{y}u-\partial_{y}^{2}u+\partial_{x}p=0,\\
&\partial_{x}u+\partial_{y}v=0,\\
&(u,v)|_{y=0}=0,\underset{y\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}(\rho,u)(x,y)=(\rho_{e}(x),u_{e}(x)),\\
&(\rho,u)|_{x=0}=(\rho_{0},u_{0})(y).\end{aligned}\right.

(1.3)

In this paper, we deal with the case

(\rho_{e}(t,x),u_{e}(t,x))=(\varrho_{\infty},u_{\infty})
, where
\varrho_{\infty}
and
u_{\infty}
are constants. It means
\partial_{x}p=0
.
We now review some related work to the system (
1.1
) and (
1.3
) in the sequence.

(I) Some results for unsteady Prandtl equations

The vanishing viscosity limit of the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations that,
in a domain with Dirichlet boundary condition, is an important problem in
both physics and mathematics.
As the viscosity coefficient tends to zero, the solution undergoes a sharp transition
from a solution of the Euler system to the zero non-slip boundary condition on boundary of the
Navier-Stokes system. This sharp transition will lead to the formation of the boundary layer.
Indeed, Prandtl
[
42
]
derived the Prandtl equations for boundary layer
from the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations with non-slip boundary condition. If the density in (
1.1
) is a constant, this system is the classical Prandtl equations.
Now, let us introduce the related matter of well-posedness results for the Prandtl equation.
If the tangential velocity field in the normal direction to the boundary satisfies
the monotonicity condition, Oleinik
[
40
,
39
]
applied the Crocco transform
to establish the global-in-time regular solutions on
[0,L]\times\mathbb{R}^{+}

for small
L
, and local-in-time solutions on
[0,L]\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
for arbitrary
large by finite
L
.
Under the monotonicity condition and a favorable pressure gradient of the Euler flow, the
global-in-time weak solutions were obtained in
[
50
]
for arbitrarily
L
by Xin and Zhang. Very recently, Xin, Zhang and Zhao
[
51
]
proved that such a weak solution is unique and in fact is a classical solution in some sense.
It should be noted that all of the above results are achieved by using Crocco transformations.
By taking care of the cancellation in the convection term to overcome the
loss of derivative in the tangential direction of velocity, the researchers
in
[
1
]
and
[
37
]
independently used the simply
energy method to establish well-posedness theory for the
two-dimensional Prandtl equations in the framework of Sobolev space.
For more results in this direction, the interested readers can refer to
the well-posedness results in the analytic or Gevrey setting without monotonicity

[
43
,
44
,
25
,
30
,
29
,
28
,
35
,
20
,
6
,
41
]
,
ill-posedness results in the Sobolev setting without monotonicity

[
4
,
7
,
34
]
, generic invalidity of boundary
layer expansions in the Sobolev spaces
[
9
,
8
,
10
,
17
,
12
,
13
,
11
]

and references therein.
It should be pointed out that most works focus on two-dimensional case, and there are only a few results in the three-dimensional case such as
[
27
,
32
,
31
,
36
]
.

(II) Some results for steady Prandtl equations

If the density in (
1.3
) is constant, this system is the classical steady Prandtl equations. It should be pointed out that the boundary conditions

\eqref{s-Prandtl}_{4}
are in themselves not enough to determine uniquely a solution
of Prandtl equations. In order to obtain the well-set problem, one should suppose
the initial data condition at the initial position
x=0
.
The initial problem of steady Prandtl equations is analogous to the initial
value problem in the theory of parabolic partial differential equations.
Thus, the coordinate
x
plays the role of time and the velocity component

u
plays the role of a temperature.
The local well-posedness result
was obtained by Oleinik
[
38
]
, who used the Von-Mises transformation
and maximum principle. Due to the degenerate property of horizontal velocity
near the boundary, it is hard to obtain the higher regularity for the steady
Prandtl equation. This problem was settled by Guo and Iyer
[
14
]

since they found the good unknown quantity (called “quotient”) to establish the closed energy estimate.
By the way, the estimates for the “quotient” play an important role in establishing the validity of the Prandtl layer expansion, see
[
15
,
24
]
.
And
[
49
]
established the global
C^{\infty}
regularity by using further maximum principle techniques despite the degeneracy near the boundary.
For the incompressible steady Navier-Stokes equations, Guo and Nguyen
[
18
]
justified the boundary layer expansion for the flow with a non-slip boundary
condition on a moving plate.
This result has been extended to the case of a rotating disk and to the case
of nonshear Euler flows(
[
21
,
22
]
).
Recently, Guo and Iyer
[
16
]
studied the boundary layer expansion for the small viscous flows with the classical no slip boundary conditions or on the static plate.
This work was extended to the case of global theory in the
x-
variable
for a large class of boundary layer with sharp decay rates.
For more results about the boundary layer expansion, the reader should consult

[
26
,
3
,
5
]
.
In terms of the asymptotic behavior of the solution, Serrin
[
45
]

used the maximum principle techniques to single out that similarity solution as those which asymptotically develop downstream, whatever may be the state of motion at the initial position at
x=0
.
In the case of localized data near the Blasius solution, Iyer
[
23
]

applied the energy method instead of maximum principle method for the good known quantity to establish specific convergence rate.
Recently Wang and Zhang
[
48
]
used the maximum principle techniques to prove the explicit decay estimate for general initial data with exponential decay.
There are many results on the steady Prandtl equations, for the dynamic stability in
[
19
]
, for the separation boundary layer in
[
2
,
46
]
.

To the best of authors’ knowledge,
there are no results concerning the well-posedness of the inhomogeneous
Prandtl equations (
1.1
) and (
1.3
).

Thus, our purpose in this paper is to establish the well-posedness result for the inhomogeneous Prandtl equations (
1.1
) and (
1.3
) respectively.
Motivated by
[
37
]
, our first target is to use the energy method to investigate the local-in-time well-posedness for the inhomogeneous unsteady Prandtl equations (
1.1
).
Motivated by the work on the homogeneous case
[
14
]
, our second target is to establish the local-in-
x
well-posedness result in higher regular Sobolev space for the inhomogeneous steady Prandtl equations (
1.3
).
Before we state our results, we give some definitions at first.

Definitions.

Now, let us define the weighted
H^{s}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma}}
,
\|\cdot\|_{H^{s}_{y,\gamma}}
and

\|\cdot\|_{\bar{H}^{s}_{y,\gamma}}
as follows,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma}}^{2}\overset{def}{=}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq s}\|\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+})}^{2};

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{s}_{y,\gamma}}^{2}\overset{def}{=}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq s}\|\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}^{2};

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\bar{H}^{s}_{y,\gamma}}^{2}\overset{def}{=}\sum_{\alpha_{2}\leq s}\|\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\partial_{y}^{\alpha_{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}^{2},

where
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
,
|\alpha|=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}
,
\partial^{\alpha}\overset{def}{=}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{y}^{\alpha_{2}}
and the weight is defined by
\langle y\rangle\overset{def}{=}1+y
.
Notice that
H_{0}^{m}\neq H^{m}
, where
H^{m}=H^{m}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+})
is the classical Sobolev space.
For any
\beta=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
, set
\beta\leq\alpha(\text{or}~\beta<\alpha)
means
|\beta|\leq|\alpha|(\text{or}~|\beta|<|\alpha|)
and
\beta_{1}\leq\alpha_{1},\beta_{2}\leq\alpha_{2}
.
We shall use the notation

C_{\alpha}^{\beta}\overset{def}{=}C_{\alpha_{1}}^{\beta_{1}}C_{\alpha_{2}}^{\beta_{2}}
to denote the quadratic coefficient,
[A,B]\overset{def}{=}AB-BA
to denote the commutator between
A
and
B
.
C_{D,E}
means the constant
C
depends on the parameters
D
and
E
. For the sake of simplicity, let us set

\int f(x,y)dxdy\overset{def}{=}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{T}}f(x,y)dxdy.

First of all, for the inhomogeneous unsteady Prandtl equations (
1.1
), let us denote

w(t,x,y)\overset{def}{=}\partial_{y}u(t,x,y)
,
\varrho(t,x,y)\overset{def}{=}\rho(t,x,y)-\varrho_{\infty}

and
w|_{t=0}=w_{0}\overset{def}{=}\partial_{y}u_{0}(x,y)
.
Define the energy norm

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\|\partial^{\alpha}w\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\varrho_{g}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|w_{g}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\!\langle y\rangle^{2\sigma_{1}+2\alpha_{2}}|\partial^{\alpha}w|^{2},

and the dissipation norm

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(t)\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{y}w_{g}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where

(g_{w},g_{\varrho})\overset{def}{=}(\frac{\partial_{y}w}{w},\frac{\partial_{y}\varrho}{w}),\quad(\varrho_{g},w_{g})\overset{def}{=}(\partial_{x}^{6}\varrho-g_{\varrho}\partial_{x}^{6}u,\partial_{x}^{6}w-g_{w}\partial_{x}^{6}u).

Therefore, our first result can be stated as follows:

Theorem 1.1

(Well-posedness result of the unsteady Prandtl equation)
.

Let the constants satisfy
\gamma>\frac{3}{2}
,
\gamma+\frac{1}{2}<\sigma_{1}\leq 2\gamma-1
,
\delta_{1}>0
and
\kappa_{2}\geq 4\kappa_{1}>0
.
For positive constants
(\varrho_{\infty},u_{\infty})
, suppose
the outer Euler flow
(\rho_{e}(t,x),u_{e}(t,x))=(\varrho_{\infty},u_{\infty})
.
Assume that the initial data satisfy

\mathcal{E}(0)<+\infty

and

\quad w_{0}(x,y)\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}}\geq 2\delta_{1},\quad 2\kappa_{1}\leq\rho_{0}(x,y)\leq\frac{\kappa_{2}}{2},

for all
(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
Then, there exist a time

0<T_{0}<<1

(depending on initial data
\mathcal{E}(0)
and parameters
\delta_{1}
,
\kappa_{1}
,
\kappa_{2}
,
\gamma
,
\sigma_{1}
)
and a unique classical solution
(\rho,u,v)
to the inhomogeneous
unsteady Prandtl equations (
1.1
) satisfying

w(t,x,y)\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}}\geq\delta_{1},\quad\quad\kappa_{1}\leq\rho(t,x,y)\leq\kappa_{2},

(1.4)

for all
(t,x,y)\in[0,T_{0}]\times\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
and

\displaystyle\underset{t\in[0,T_{0}]}{\sup}\mathcal{E}(t)+\int_{0}^{T_{0}}\mathcal{D}(t)dt\leq 2\mathcal{E}(0).

(1.5)

Remark 1.1
.

Due to the relation
w\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\in L^{2}
and
w\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}}\geq\delta_{1}
,
we need to require
\sigma_{1}
and
\gamma
satisfy the relation
\sigma_{1}>\gamma+\frac{1}{2}
.
In order to control the nonlinear term in density equation(see estimate (
3.25
)),
we require
\sigma_{1}\leq 2\gamma-1
. Thus, the weight index
\sigma_{1}
should satisfy

\gamma+\frac{1}{2}<\sigma_{1}\leq 2\gamma-1
.

Remark 1.2
.

The outer constant Euler flow will give rise to the important boundary condition

\partial_{y}w|_{y=0}=0
. This boundary condition will play an important role
as we deal with the nonlinear term
I_{4}
(see (
3.18
)).
Therefore, the local-in-time well-posedness in Theorem
1.1

holds only for the case of outer constant Euler flow.

Remark 1.3
.

For the sake of simplicity, the local-in-time existence and uniqueness results
in Theorem
1.1
are established only under the
H^{6}
-framework.
However, it should be pointed out that one can establish
similar result in more higher regular Sobolev space.

Next, for the inhomogeneous steady Prandtl equations (
1.3
), we denote
\bar{\rho}(x,y)\overset{def}{=}\rho(x,y)-\varrho_{\infty}
and
\bar{u}(x,y)\overset{def}{=}u(x,y)-u_{\infty}
.
Let us define the energy norm

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}(x)\overset{def}{=}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|\partial^{\alpha}\bar{\rho}\|_{L_{y}^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|\sqrt{\rho}u\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{y}(\frac{v}{u})\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{2}}\|_{L_{y}^{2}}^{2}+\|\bar{u}\|_{L_{y}^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{y}\bar{u}\|_{L_{y}^{2}}^{2},

and the dissipation norm

\mathcal{Y}(x)\overset{def}{=}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|\sqrt{u}\partial_{y}^{2}\partial^{\alpha}(\frac{v}{u})\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{2}}\|_{L_{y}^{2}}^{2}.

Our second result can be stated as follows:

Theorem 1.2

(Well-posedness result of the steady Prandtl equation)
.

Let the constants satisfy
m\geq 3
,
\sigma_{2}\geq 2
and
\kappa_{3}>0
.
For positive constants
(\varrho_{\infty},u_{\infty})
, suppose
the outer Euler flow
(\rho_{e}(x),u_{e}(x))=(\varrho_{\infty},u_{\infty})
.
Assume
(\rho_{0}(y)-\varrho_{\infty},u_{0}(y)-u_{\infty})\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})

decays fast and satisfies the following conditions:

u_{0}(y)>0~~\text{for~all}~y>0,\quad u_{0}(0)=0,\quad\lim_{y\rightarrow+\infty}u_{0}(y)=u_{\infty},\quad u_{0}^{\prime}(0)>0,\quad u_{0}^{\prime\prime}(0)=0,

(1.6)

and for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

\rho_{0}(y)\geq\kappa_{3}.

(1.7)

Suppose also the generic compatibility conditions hold at the corner
(0,0)

up to order
m
. Then there exists a time
0<L_{a}<<1

(depending on initial data
\rho_{0}
,
u_{0}
and parameters
m
,
\sigma_{2}
,
\kappa_{3}
)
and a unique classical solution
(\rho,u,v)
to the inhomogeneous
steady Prandtl equations (
1.3
) satisfying

u(x,y)>0,\quad\rho(x,y)\geq\kappa_{3},

(1.8)

for all
(x,y)\in[0,L_{a}]\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
and

\displaystyle\underset{x\in[0,L_{a}]}{\sup}\mathcal{X}(x)+\int_{0}^{L_{a}}{\mathcal{Y}(x)}dx\leq 2C_{\rho_{0},u_{0}}.

(1.9)

Remark 1.4
.

Although the initial data of vertical velocity is not given,
one can give the initial control of
\mathcal{X}(0)
in terms of
\rho_{0}
and
u_{0}
.
To achieve the target, we need the generic compatibility conditions hold at the
corner
(0,0)
up to order
m
, see Appendix
D
in detail.
Under the conditions of Theorem
1.2
,
then we can apply the equation (
1.3
) to obtain the following estimates:

\|u-u_{\infty}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{y}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{y}^{2}u\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{y}^{\alpha_{2}}(\rho-\varrho_{\infty})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{m,\sigma_{2},\kappa_{3},\rho_{0},u_{0}},

for all
0\leq\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\leq m
.

The rest of the paper is organized as follows.
In Section
2
, we explain the difficulties and our approach to establish the well-posedness theory for the inhomogeneous Prandtl equations (
1.1
) and (
1.3
) respectively.
In Section
3
and
4
, we prove the local-in-time well-posedness of (
1.1
) in weighted Sobolev spaces by energy methods. Precisely, in Section
3
, we derive a priori estimate and
obtain the closed energy estimates of the regularized inhomogeneous Prandtl system (
2.1
).
Finally, the well-posedness result for the original Prandtl equations (
1.1
) will be
investigated in Section
4
.
In Sections
5
and
6
, the
local-in-
x
well-posedness of the inhomogeneous steady Prandtl equations (
1.3
) in higher regular Sobolev space is established.

2
Difficulties and outline of our approach

In this section, we will explain the main difficulties of proving Theorems
1.1
and
1.2
as well as our strategies for overcoming them.

2.1.  Difficulties and outline of our approach for the inhomogeneous unsteady Prandtl system

First of all, similar to the classical Prandtl equations, the main difficulty of Prandtl system (
1.1
) comes from the loss of tangential derivative
of horizontal velocity, therefore the standard energy estimate can not apply.
Without using the classical Crocco transformation as well as other change of variables, Masmoudi and Wong
[
37
]
introduce a new weighted
H^{s}
-norm to avoid the regularity loss created by the vertical velocity
v
through a cancellation property.
Since the outer Euler flow
(\rho_{e},u_{e})
is independent of variables
x
and
t
,
motivated by their idea, we consider the following approximated system

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\varrho^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}\varrho^{\varepsilon}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\varrho^{\varepsilon}=0,\\
&\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}u^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}=0,\\
&\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{y}v^{\varepsilon}=0,\\
&(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon})|_{y=0}=0,\underset{y\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}(\varrho^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})(t,x,y)=(0,u_{\infty}),\\
&(\varrho^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})|_{t=0}\overset{def}{=}(\rho_{0}-\varrho_{\infty},u_{0})(x,y),\end{aligned}\right.

(2.1)

where
\varrho^{\varepsilon}\overset{def}{=}\rho^{\varepsilon}-\varrho_{\infty}
. Let us define
w^{\varepsilon}\overset{def}{=}\partial_{y}u^{\varepsilon}
,
and taking
\partial_{y}
-operator to the equation
\eqref{Prandtl-01}_{2}
, we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}w^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}-\partial_{y}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon})=0,\\
&\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}|_{y=0}=0,\\
&w^{\varepsilon}|_{t=0}\overset{def}{=}\partial_{y}u_{0}(x,y).\end{aligned}\right.

(2.2)

It should be pointed out that the nonlinear terms
v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}

and
v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}
will create loss of tangential derivative.
Similar to
[
37
]
, taking
\partial_{x}^{6}
-operator to the density equation
and velocity equation respectively, we have

(\partial_{t}+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y})\partial_{x}^{6}\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{x}^{6}v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\partial_{x}^{6}\varrho^{\varepsilon}+...,

(2.3)

and

(\partial_{t}+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y})\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{x}^{6}v^{\varepsilon}w^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{8}u^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{x}^{6}\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\}+...,

(2.4)

where the symbol … represents the lower-order terms that
we want the reader to ignore at this moment.
The main obstacle in both (
2.3
) and (
2.4
) is the term
\partial_{x}^{6}v^{\varepsilon}=-\partial_{x}^{6}\partial_{y}^{-1}u^{\varepsilon}
, which has
7
x
-derivatives so that the standard energy estimates cannot apply.
However, we can eliminate the problematic term by subtracting them
in an appropriate way by using the combination of equations (
2.3
) and (
2.4
).
Subtracting
g_{\varrho}^{\varepsilon}(\overset{def}{=}\frac{\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}})\times
(
2.4
) from (
2.3
), we obtain

\partial_{t}\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}=2\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{7}u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}g_{\varrho}^{\varepsilon}+...,

where the function
\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}\overset{def}{=}\partial_{x}^{6}\varrho^{\varepsilon}-g_{\varrho}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}
.
This good quantity
\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}
will avoid the loss of tangential derivative.
Similarly, in order to deal with the term
\partial_{x}^{6}v^{\varepsilon}w^{\varepsilon}
, we establish
the following equation

\partial_{t}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{y}\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\}+...,

where
w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\overset{def}{=}\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}-g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}

and
g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\overset{def}{=}\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}
.
Instead of estimating the weighted norm of
(\partial_{x}^{6}\varrho^{\varepsilon},\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon})
,
we estimate that of
(\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon},w_{g}^{\varepsilon})
with the same weight
\gamma
to close the energy estimate, which can avoid the loss of
x
-variable thanks to some cancellation mechanism.

Secondly, since the domain
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
is unbounded,
we use the Hardy-type inequalities (see (
A.1
) and (
A.2
))
to control the quantities
u^{\varepsilon}
and
v^{\varepsilon}=-\int^{y}_{0}\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}dy^{\prime}

by the vorticity
w^{\varepsilon}
.
Since it will generate some space weight, we add the suitable weights respectively to vorticity and density, including the weight
\langle y\rangle
for each
y
-variable.
In order to close the energy estimate, it remains to derive the weighted
L^{\infty}
controls on the lower-order derivatives of
w^{\varepsilon}
and
\varrho^{\varepsilon}

with the same weight
\sigma_{1}
.
On the one hand, we view the equation of
\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}
as a “linear” parabolic equations and control the lower-order derivatives of
w^{\varepsilon}

by using the classical maximum principle.
On the other hand, we use the Sobolev embedding inequality and good estimate
of transport equation to control the lower-order derivatives
of
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
with weight
\sigma_{1}
.
Then, we require the lower order derivative of density in energy norm
is along with the weight
\sigma_{1}
.
This weight, as we deal with the term
I_{8}
(see estimate (
3.25
)),
needs us to require the index condition
\sigma_{1}\leq 2\gamma-1
.
The weights should be chosen carefully so that
we can establish the closed energy estimate.

Finally, the lack of high-order boundary conditions prevents us from using the integration by part in the
y
-variable. However, when the highest order of derivative is even, one can use the trace estimate (
A.4
) and the equation
\eqref{Prandtl-02}_{1}
to reduce
the order of derivatives (see the term
I_{6}
).
However, due to the appearance of density, this procedure is somewhat complicated.
Thus, we only consider the
H^{6}
-framework in our paper just for the sake of simplicity.
Therefore, the local-in-time well-posedness for the original system (
1.1
) can be obtained directly as the parameter
\varepsilon
tends to
0^{+}
.

2.2.  Difficulties and outline of our approach for
the inhomogeneous steady Prandtl system

For the case of homogeneous flow, the main difficulty of steady Prandtl
equation arises from the loss of tangential derivative of horizontal velocity.
Thus, with the help of Von-Mises transformation, Oleinik and Samokhin
[
40
]

established the local-in-
x
well-posedness in lower regular space.
Due to the degenerate property of horizontal velocity on the boundary,
it fails to establish the higher regularity of solution.
In this respect, Guo and Iyer
[
14
]
have settled this open problem since they found
the good quotient
\partial_{y}(\frac{v}{\bar{u}})
. Here
\bar{u}
is the classical Prandtl solution obtained by Oleinik and Samokhin
[
40
]
.

Now let us focus on the local-in-
x
well-posedness of inhomogeneous
steady Prandtl system (
1.3
).
Motivated by the idea by Guo and Iyer
[
14
]
, we rewrite the original system
as follows

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{x}\rho+q\partial_{y}\rho=0,\\
&\partial_{x}(\rho u^{2}\partial_{y}q)-\partial_{y}^{3}(uq)=0,\\
&\partial_{x}u+\partial_{y}(uq)=0,\end{aligned}\right.

(2.5)

where the quotient
q\overset{def}{=}\frac{v}{u}
.
From the Eqs.
\eqref{e:rewrite}_{1}
and
\eqref{e:rewrite}_{2}
, one can establish some estimate
for the density
\rho
and quotient
\partial_{y}(\frac{v}{u})
.
However, in order to close the energy estimate, we need to provide estimate for
the horizontal velocity
u
. Indeed, due to assumption of positive initial
vorticity on the boundary, the horizontal velocity
u
is equivalent to
y

near the boundary. Thus, it only needs to give the estimate for the far field
component of horizontal velocity
u
.
As mentioned by Serrin
[
45
]
, the classical steady Prandtl equation
is acting like an evolution equation, with
x
being a time-like variable,
y
being space-like.

Thus, the divergence-free condition is essentially an evolution equation of
horizontal velocity
u
.
Then, one can establish the estimate for horizontal velocity
u
to
establish closed a priori energy estimate.

Finally, we point out that we need to control the term
q
by
\partial_{y}q
by using Hardy inequality. Since it will generate one space weight, our method here is to add the suitable weight
\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{2}}
to the quantity
\partial_{y}q
.
Therefore, we can establish the local-in-
x
well-posedness of inhomogeneous
steady Prandtl equation in higher regular space.

3
A priori estimate of the approximated unsteady
Prandtl equations

In this section, we will establish a priori estimate for the
regularized inhomogeneous Prandtl system (
2.1
).
First of all, we define the energy norms

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}|\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}|^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}(t)\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 6}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{x}^{6}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}|\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}|^{2}.

Then we define the dissipation norm

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}(t)\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\varepsilon\|\partial_{x}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\varepsilon\|\partial_{x}\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{x}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}}\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{x}\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Now, let us state the following Proposition.

Proposition 3.1
.

Assume
\gamma>\frac{3}{2},\gamma+\frac{1}{2}<\sigma_{1}\leq 2\gamma-1
,
\delta>0
,
0<\kappa\leq\bar{\kappa}

and
\mathcal{E}_{1}(0)<+\infty
.
Suppose

w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}}\geq\delta,\quad\kappa\leq\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}\leq\bar{\kappa},

(3.1)

holds for all
(t,x,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
Then, it holds for all
T_{0}\leq T

\displaystyle E_{1}(T_{0})+\int_{0}^{T_{0}}\mathcal{D}_{1}(\tau)d\tau\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(0)+C_{\bar{\kappa},\kappa,\delta,\gamma,\sigma_{1}}(1+E_{1}(T_{0})^{20})T_{0}

(3.2)

\displaystyle+(\|I(0)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+})}+C_{\kappa}T_{0}(1+E_{1}(T_{0})^{4}))e^{C_{\sigma_{1},\kappa}(1+E_{1}(T_{0})^{2})T_{0}},

where
E_{1}(T)\overset{def}{=}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)
.

3.1.  Weighted estimates with normal derivatives

Before we obtain the weighted estimates with normal derivatives for the vorticity and density, we need to investigate the estimates as follows, which will be used frequently to deal with the nonlinear term.

Lemma 3.2
.

Under the condition of (
3.1
),
the solution
(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon},w^{\varepsilon})
of regularized system
(
2.1
) will satisfy

\mathcal{E}_{2}(t)\leq C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{3}),

(3.3)

and

\|\partial_{y}\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{4}).

(3.4)

Proof.

First of all, it is easy to check that

w_{g}^{\varepsilon}=\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}=w^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}(\frac{\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}).

(3.5)

Using relation (
3.5
), the Hardy inequality (
A.2
)
and lower bound condition (
3.1
), it holds

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-\sigma_{1}-1}\|_{L^{2}}

(3.6)

\displaystyle\leq\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{y}\left(\frac{\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}\right)\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-\sigma_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\frac{w_{g}^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-\sigma_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}(1+\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}).

Due to the relation

\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}=\partial_{x}^{6}\varrho^{\varepsilon}-g_{\varrho}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}=\partial_{x}^{6}\varrho^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}g_{\varrho}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}
,
using the Hardy inequality (
A.2
)
we can obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{6}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+\|g_{\varrho}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

(3.7)

\displaystyle\leq\|\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{\delta}\|\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+C_{\gamma,\delta}\|\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{H^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varrho_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}\|\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{H^{2}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}(1+\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}),

where we have used (
3.6
) in the last inequality.
The combination of estimates (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) yields directly

\mathcal{E}_{2}(t)\leq C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{3}).

Finally, due to the relation (
3.5
) and
lower bound condition (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}(w_{g}^{\varepsilon}+\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon})\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\frac{\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}w^{\varepsilon}-(\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(w^{\varepsilon})^{2}}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+C_{\delta}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{\delta,\gamma}(\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+2}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})\|\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}},

which, together with estimate (
3.6
), yields directly

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{2}).

Therefore, we complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Now we first establish the weighted estimates with normal derivatives for the vorticity.

Lemma 3.3
.

Under the condition of (
3.1
),
the solution
(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon},w^{\varepsilon})
of regularized system
(
2.1
) will satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\!\!\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\!\!\!\!\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\varepsilon\|\partial_{x}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\!\!\!\!\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(3.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\nu_{1}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu_{2}\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\kappa,\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{20}),

where
\nu_{1}
and
\nu_{2}
are the small constants that will be chosen later.

Proof.

For any
|\alpha|\leq 6
and
\alpha_{1}\leq 5
,
applying the
\partial^{\alpha}
differential operator to the
equation
\eqref{Prandtl-02}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon})+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}(\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon})+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}(\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}(\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon})-\partial_{y}\left\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}

(3.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-[\partial^{\alpha},u^{\varepsilon}]\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}-[\partial^{\alpha},v^{\varepsilon}]\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{y}\left\{\partial^{\alpha}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}+\partial_{y}\left\{\sum_{0<\beta<\alpha}C^{\alpha}_{\beta}\partial^{\beta}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}.

Multiplying (
3.9
) by
\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}

and integrating over
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we have

\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy+\varepsilon\int|\partial_{x}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 6}I_{i},

(3.10)

where the terms
I_{i}(1\leq i\leq 6)
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle I_{1}=(\gamma+\alpha_{2})\int u^{\varepsilon}_{2}|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}-1}dxdy;

\displaystyle I_{2}=-\int[\partial^{\alpha},u^{\varepsilon}]\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy;

\displaystyle I_{3}=-\int[\partial^{\alpha},v^{\varepsilon}]\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy;

\displaystyle I_{4}=\int\partial_{y}\left\{\partial^{\alpha}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy;

\displaystyle I_{5}=\sum_{0<\beta<\alpha}C^{\alpha}_{\beta}\int\partial_{y}\left\{\partial^{\beta}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy;

\displaystyle I_{6}=\int\partial_{y}\left\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy.

In the sequence, we will give the estimate from terms
I_{1}
to
I_{6}
.

Deal with term
I_{1}
.

It is easy to check that

|I_{1}|\leq C_{\gamma}\|v^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\gamma}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\gamma}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{2}),

(3.11)

where we have used the following estimate

\displaystyle\|v^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|v^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2}\left(v^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\right)\|_{L^{2}}\right)

(3.12)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\partial_{y}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{xy}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{1}}.

Here we have used Hardy inequality (
A.1
) and the Sobolev inequality (
A.3
).

Deal with term
I_{2}
.

For
i=1,2
, using Hardy inequality (
A.1
) and the Morse-type inequality (
A.5
), we have

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\beta}\int\partial^{\beta-e_{i}}\partial^{e_{i}}u^{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy|

(3.13)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial^{e_{i}}u^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{i-1}\|_{H^{5}_{0}}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial^{e_{i}}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{i}\|_{H^{5}_{0}}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{4}),

where we have used the estimate (
3.3
) in the last inequality.

Deal with term
I_{3}
.

If
\beta_{2}>0
, the divergence-free condition
\eqref{Prandtl-01}_{3}
yields directly

\displaystyle|I_{3}|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\beta}\int\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{y}^{\beta_{2}-1}\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy|

(3.14)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{0}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma+1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{1}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma+1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}.

If
\beta_{2}=0
, we have for all
|\alpha|\leq 5
,

\displaystyle|I_{3}|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\beta}\int\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}-1}\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy|

(3.15)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{-1}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\gamma+1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{0}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\gamma+1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{1}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\gamma+1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}.

If
\beta_{2}=0
and
|\alpha|=6
, then it implies
\alpha_{2}\geq 1
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle|I_{3}|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\beta}\int\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}-1}\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha-\beta-e_{2}}\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy|

(3.16)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{-1}}\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\gamma+2}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{1}}\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\gamma+2}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}.

Thus, the combination of estimates (
3.14
)-(
3.16
)
and (
3.3
) yields directly

|I_{3}|\leq C_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{3}).

(3.17)

Deal with term
I_{4}
.

Integrating by part and applying the boundary condition
\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}|_{y=0}=0
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{4}|=\Big|

\displaystyle\int\partial^{\alpha}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dx

(3.18)

\displaystyle+(2\gamma+2\alpha_{2})\int\partial^{\alpha}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}-1}dx\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\gamma}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial^{\alpha}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{\gamma}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial^{\alpha}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}-1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\gamma,\kappa}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}(1+\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{6}_{\gamma}}^{6})\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{\gamma,\kappa}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}(1+\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{6}_{\gamma}}^{6})\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}-1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\nu_{1}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{1},\kappa,\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{19}),

where we have used the estimate (
3.3
) in the last inequality.

Deal with term
I_{5}
.

Integrating by part and applying the boundary condition
\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}|_{y=0}=0
, we have

\displaystyle I_{5}=

\displaystyle\sum_{0<\beta<\alpha}C^{\alpha}_{\beta}\int\partial_{y}\left\{\partial^{\beta}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0<\beta<\alpha}C^{\alpha}_{\beta}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\partial^{\beta}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\right)_{y=0}dx-\int\partial^{\beta}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy\right)

\displaystyle-(2\gamma+2\alpha_{2})\sum_{0<\beta<\alpha}C^{\alpha}_{\beta}\int\partial^{\beta}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}-1}dxdy

\displaystyle\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle I_{5,1}+I_{5,2}+I_{5,3}.

Similar to the term
I_{4}
, for any
i=1,2
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{5,2}|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\langle y\rangle^{i-1}\partial^{e_{i}}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\|_{H^{5}_{0}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\kappa}(1+\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{6}_{0}}^{6})\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{1}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{1},\kappa,\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{19});

\displaystyle|I_{5,3}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\gamma}\|\langle y\rangle^{i-1}\partial^{e_{i}}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\|_{H^{5}_{0}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\gamma,\kappa}(1+\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{6}_{0}}^{6})\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\gamma,\kappa,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{10}).

Using the trace inequality (
A.4
),

\displaystyle|I_{5,1}|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{0<\beta<\alpha}C(\|\partial^{\beta}\partial_{y}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial^{\beta}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial^{\beta}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\kappa}(1+\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{6}}^{6})\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+C_{\kappa}(1+\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}^{5})\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{\kappa}(1+\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}^{5})\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\nu_{2}\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{2},\kappa,\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{10}).

The combination of estimates from terms
I_{5,1}
to
I_{5,3}
gives directly

|I_{5}|\leq\nu_{1}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu_{2}\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\kappa,\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{19}).

(3.19)

Deal with term
I_{6}
.

Indeed, integrating by part, we have

\displaystyle I_{6}=

\displaystyle\underset{I_{6,1}}{\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\right)_{y=0}dx}}-\int\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy

\displaystyle-\underset{I_{6,2}}{\underbrace{\int\frac{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma+2\alpha_{2}-1}dxdy}}.

Using the Hölder inequality, we have

|I_{6,2}|\leq C_{\gamma}\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}-1}\|_{L^{2}}.

Let us deal with the boundary term
I_{6,1}
.

Deal with the case:
|\alpha|\leq 5
.

The application of boundary embedding inequality (
A.4
) yields

\displaystyle|I_{6,1}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(\|\partial_{y}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}^{2}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}})\|\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}}\partial_{y}^{2}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\mu,\kappa}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{2}).

The combination of
I_{6,1}
and
I_{6,2}
yields directly

\displaystyle I_{6}\leq(\mu-1)\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\mu,\kappa,\gamma}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{2}).

(3.20)

Deal with the case:
|\alpha|=6
.

Case 1:
\alpha_{2}
is even.

For
\alpha_{2}=2k(k=1,2,3)
, we can apply the vorticity
equation
\eqref{Prandtl-02}_{1}
to obtain

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{y}^{3}w^{\varepsilon}|_{y=0}=&((\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty})w^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon})_{y=0}+\left(\frac{2\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\right)_{y=0},\\
\partial_{y}^{5}w^{\varepsilon}|_{y=0}=&N_{1},\\
\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}^{7}w^{\varepsilon}|_{y=0}=&(\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty})N_{2}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}N_{1}+\left(\partial_{y}^{5}(u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon})\right)_{y=0}\\
&-\left(\partial_{y}^{k}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{y}^{7-k}w^{\varepsilon}\right)_{y=0}.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.21)

where the functions
N_{i}(i=1,2)
are defined in (
B.5
) and (
B.23
).
The boundary reduction relation (
3.21
)
can be proven in Appendix
B
.
Thus, for
k=1,2,3
, it is easy to check that

\displaystyle|I_{6,1}|=

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{x}^{6-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+1}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{6-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k}w^{\varepsilon}\right)_{y=0}dx|\leq\nu_{2}\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{2},\kappa,\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{20}).

Case 2:
\alpha_{2}
is odd.

Then,
\alpha_{1}\leq 5
and
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}=6
implies
\alpha_{2}\geq 1
.
Obviously, if
\alpha_{2}=1
, then the condition
\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}|_{y=0}=0
yields directly

I_{6,1}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{x}^{5}\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{5}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\right)_{y=0}dx=0.

Thus, for
\alpha_{2}=2k+1(k=1,2)
, we integrate by part
with respect to
x
to obtain

\displaystyle I_{6,1}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{x}^{5-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+2}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{5-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+1}w^{\varepsilon}\right)_{y=0}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\partial_{x}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{x}^{4-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+2}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{5-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+1}w^{\varepsilon}\right)_{y=0}dx

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{x}^{4-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+2}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{6-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+1}w^{\varepsilon}\right)_{y=0}dx

\displaystyle\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle I_{6,1,1}+I_{6,1,2}.

Using the trace inequality (
A.4
), we have

\displaystyle|I_{6,1,1}|\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{x}^{4-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{5-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{y}\left\{\partial_{x}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{x}^{4-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+2}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{5-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+1}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\kappa}\|\partial_{x}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{0}}(\|\partial_{x}^{4-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{5-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}^{4-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+3}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{5-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+1}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle+C_{\kappa}(1+\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{0}}^{2})\|\partial_{x}^{4-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{5-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+1}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\nu_{2}\|\partial_{x}^{4-2k}\partial_{y}^{2k+3}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{2},\kappa}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{2}).

For
k=1,2
, the quantity
\partial_{y}^{2k+1}\partial_{x}^{6-2k}w^{\varepsilon}|_{y=0}

in term
I_{6,1,1}
has an odd number of
y
derivatives,
and hence, we can apply the boundary reduction equality

\eqref{bl-redu}_{1}
and
\eqref{bl-redu}_{2}
to further reduce
the order of derivative in term
I_{6,1,1}
. Then, similar to the
case 1, we can apply the trace inequality (
A.4
) to obtain

\displaystyle|I_{6,1,2}|\leq\nu_{2}\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{2},\kappa,\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{20}).

The combination of estimates from
I_{6,1,1}
and
I_{6,1,2}
yields directly

\displaystyle|I_{6,1}|\leq\nu_{2}\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{2},\kappa,\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{20}),

which, together with the estimate of
I_{6,2}
, yields directly

\displaystyle I_{6}\leq\nu_{2}\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}}^{2}+(\mu-1)\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}}\partial_{y}\partial^{\alpha}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{2},\mu,\kappa,\gamma,\sigma_{1},\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{20}).

(3.22)

Substituting the estimates to (
3.11
), (
3.13
), (
3.17
)-(
3.20
) and (
3.22
) into (
3.10
) and choosing
\mu
small enough, we can obtain the estimate (
3.8
).
Therefore, we complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Next, we establish the weighted estimates with normal derivatives for the density.

Lemma 3.4
.

Under the condition of (
3.1
),
the solution
(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon},w^{\varepsilon})
of regularized system
(
2.1
) will satisfy

\frac{d}{dt}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\frac{1}{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 6,\alpha_{1}\leq 5}\varepsilon\|\partial_{x}\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\sigma_{1},\gamma,\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{4}).

Proof.

For any
|\alpha|\leq 6
and
\alpha_{1}\leq 5
,
applying the
\partial^{\alpha}
differential operator to the
equation
\eqref{Prandtl-01}_{1}
, we have

\partial_{t}(\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon})+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}(\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon})+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}(\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}(\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon})=-[\partial^{\alpha},u^{\varepsilon}]\partial_{x}\varrho^{\varepsilon}-[\partial^{\alpha},v^{\varepsilon}]\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}.

Applying this equation by
\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\sigma_{1}+2\alpha_{2}}
and integrating
over
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{x}\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\underset{I_{7}}{\underbrace{(\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2})\int|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}v^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\sigma_{1}+2\alpha_{2}-1}dxdy}}

(3.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\underset{I_{8}}{\underbrace{\int[\partial^{\alpha},u^{\varepsilon}]\partial_{x}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\sigma_{1}+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy}}-\underset{I_{9}}{\underbrace{\int[\partial^{\alpha},v^{\varepsilon}]\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\sigma_{1}+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy}}.

Using the estimate (
3.12
), one has

\displaystyle|I_{7}|\leq C_{\sigma_{1}}\|v^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\sigma_{1}}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\sigma_{1}}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{2}).

(3.24)

For
i=1,2
, one can apply the Morse-type inequality (
A.5
), Hardy inequality (
A.1
) and
\sigma_{1}\leq 2\gamma-1
to obtain

\displaystyle|I_{8}|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}C^{\alpha}_{\beta}\int\partial^{\beta-e_{i}}\partial^{e_{i}}u^{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{x}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\sigma_{1}+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy|

(3.25)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial^{e_{i}}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\sigma_{1}-\gamma+i-1}}\|\partial_{x}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\sigma_{1},\gamma,\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{4}),

where we have used the estimate (
3.3
).
Finally, let us deal with the term
I_{9}
.
If
\beta_{2}>0
, then we apply the divergence free condition

\eqref{Prandtl-01}_{3}
and Morse-type inequality (
A.5
) to obtain

\displaystyle|I_{9}|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\beta}\int\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{y}^{\beta_{2}-1}\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\sigma_{1}+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{0}}\|\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\sigma_{1}+1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\sigma_{1},\gamma,\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{3}),

where we have used Hardy inequality (
A.1
)
and estimate (
3.3
).
If
\beta_{2}=0
, we have for all
|\alpha|\leq 5
,

\displaystyle|I_{9}|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\beta}\int\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}-e_{1}}\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\sigma_{1}+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{-1}}\|\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\sigma_{1}+1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{0}}\|\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\sigma_{1}+1}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\sigma_{1},\gamma,\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{3}),

where we have used the Hardy inequalities
(
A.1
), (
A.2
) and estimate (
3.3
).
If
\beta_{2}=0
and
|\alpha|=6
, then it implies
\alpha_{2}\geq 1
, and we have

\displaystyle|I_{9}|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{\beta}\int\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}-e_{1}}\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha-\beta-e_{2}}\partial_{y}^{2}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\sigma_{1}+2\alpha_{2}}dxdy|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{-1}}\|\partial_{y}^{2}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\sigma_{1}+2}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{0}}\|\partial_{y}^{2}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\sigma_{1}+2}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\sigma_{1},\gamma,\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{3}).

Thus, the term
I_{9}
can be estimated as follows

|I_{9}|\leq C_{\sigma_{1},\gamma,\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{3}).

(3.26)

Substituting the estimates (
3.24
), (
3.25
)
and (
3.26
) into (
3.23
), we have

\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{x}\partial^{\alpha}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+\alpha_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\sigma_{1},\gamma,\delta}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{4}).

Therefore, we complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

3.2.   Weighted estimates only in tangential Variables

First of all, we establish the estimate of only tangential derivative of vorticity.
Applying
\partial_{x}^{6}
-operator to the vorticity
equation
\eqref{Prandtl-02}_{1}
, we have

\partial_{t}(\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon})+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}(\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon})+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}(\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{x}^{6}v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{8}w^{\varepsilon}-\partial_{x}^{6}\partial_{y}\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\}=Q_{1},

(3.27)

where the function
Q_{1}
is defined as follows

Q_{1}\overset{def}{=}-\sum_{0<k\leq 6}C_{6}^{k}\partial_{x}^{k}u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{7-k}w^{\varepsilon}-\sum_{0<k<6}C_{6}^{k}\partial_{x}^{k}v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{6-k}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}.

Applying
\partial_{x}^{6}
-operator to the velocity
equation
\eqref{Prandtl-01}_{2}
, we have

\partial_{t}(\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon})+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}(\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon})+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}(\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{x}^{6}v^{\varepsilon}w^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{8}u^{\varepsilon}-\partial_{x}^{6}\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\}=Q_{2},

(3.28)

where the function
Q_{2}
is defined as follows

Q_{2}\overset{def}{=}-\sum_{0<k\leq 6}C_{6}^{k}\partial_{x}^{k}u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{7-k}u^{\varepsilon}-\sum_{0<k<6}C_{6}^{k}\partial_{x}^{k}v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{6-k}w^{\varepsilon}.

The most difficult term in both (
3.27
) and (
3.28
)
is
\partial_{x}^{6}v^{\varepsilon}=-\partial_{y}^{-1}\partial_{x}^{7}u^{\varepsilon}
,
which causes the loss of
x
-derivative of horizontal velocity,
so that the standard energy estimate cannot apply.
To eliminate the problematic term
\partial_{x}^{6}v^{\varepsilon}
, we subtract the equations (
3.27
) and (
3.28
) in an appropriate way under the condition of (
3.1
) which makes sure that
w^{\varepsilon}>0
.
Recall that
g_{w}^{\varepsilon}=\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}
, then multiplying (
3.28
) by
g_{w}^{\varepsilon}
, it holds

\partial_{t}(\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon})g_{w}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}(\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon})g_{w}^{\varepsilon}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}(\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon})g_{w}^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{x}^{6}v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{8}u^{\varepsilon}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}-\partial_{x}^{6}\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}=Q_{2}\cdot g_{w}^{\varepsilon}.

(3.29)

Then, recall that
w_{g}^{\varepsilon}=\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}-g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}=\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}
, it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}+v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}-\partial_{y}\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\}=Q_{1}-Q_{2}\cdot g_{w}^{\varepsilon}-Q_{3}-Q_{4}\cdot\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon},

(3.30)

here
Q_{i}(i=3,4)
are defined as follows.

\displaystyle Q_{3}\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle-\partial_{y}\left\{\sum_{0<k\leq 6}C_{6}^{k}\partial_{x}^{k}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{x}^{6-k}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}-\partial_{y}\left\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle-\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\}+\sum_{0<k\leq 6}C_{6}^{k}\partial_{x}^{k}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{x}^{6-k}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}-2\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{7}u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}g_{w}^{\varepsilon};

\displaystyle Q_{4}\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{w^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{y}\left\{-u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}-v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{y}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon})\right\}-\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}}{(w^{\varepsilon})^{2}}\left(-u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}-v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{y}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon})\right)

\displaystyle+u^{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\partial_{xy}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}-\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}}{(w^{\varepsilon})^{2}}\right)+v^{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}-\frac{(\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(w^{\varepsilon})^{2}}\right)-\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}(\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}})+\varepsilon\frac{\partial_{y}\partial_{x}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}}{w^{\varepsilon}}-\varepsilon\frac{\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}}{(w^{\varepsilon})^{2}}.

The above equation (
3.30
) is proven in
Appendix
C
in detail.
Now, we are going to establish the weighted estimate for
this good quantity
w_{g}^{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Under the condition of (
3.1
),
the solution
(u^{\varepsilon},v^{\varepsilon},w^{\varepsilon})
of regularized system
(
2.1
) will satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{x}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}}\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\nu_{3}\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{3},\kappa,\delta,\gamma,\sigma_{1}}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{19}),

where
\nu_{3}
is the small constant that will be chosen later.

Proof.

Multiplying the equation (
3.30
) by
w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}
and
integrating over
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int(w_{g}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy+\varepsilon\int|\partial_{x}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy+\int\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy

(3.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{J_{1}}{\underbrace{\gamma\int v^{\varepsilon}(w_{g}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma-1}dxdy}}-\underset{J_{2}}{\underbrace{\int\frac{2\gamma}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma-1}dxdy}}+\underset{J_{3}}{\underbrace{\int Q_{1}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy}}

\displaystyle-\underset{J_{4}}{\underbrace{\int Q_{2}\cdot g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy}}-\underset{J_{5}}{\underbrace{\int Q_{3}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy}}-\underset{J_{6}}{\underbrace{\int Q_{4}\cdot\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy}}.

Using the estimate (
3.12
), we have

|J_{1}|\leq C_{\gamma}\|v^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\gamma}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{1}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

(3.32)

and

|J_{2}|\leq C_{\gamma,\kappa}\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\nu_{3}\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{3},\gamma,\kappa}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(3.33)

Deal with
J_{3}
and
J_{4}
terms.

Using Hardy inequality (
A.1
), the Morse-type inequality (
A.5
)
and estimate (
3.3
), we have

\displaystyle|J_{3}|\leq

\displaystyle|\sum_{1\leq k\leq 6}C_{6}^{k}\int\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{6-k}\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy|

(3.34)

\displaystyle+|\sum_{1\leq k\leq 5}C_{6}^{k}\int\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{5-k}\partial_{xy}w^{\varepsilon}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{0}}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{-1}}\|\partial_{xy}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\gamma+1}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{1}}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\partial_{x}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{1}}\|\partial_{xy}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\gamma+1}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\gamma,\delta,\sigma_{1}}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{4}),

and using the lower bound condition (
3.1
), we arrive at

\displaystyle|J_{4}|\leq

\displaystyle C_{\delta}\|\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{0}}\|\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma-1}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

(3.35)

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}\|\partial_{x}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{-1}}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\gamma}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\delta}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{1}}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}\|\partial_{x}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{1}}\|\partial_{x}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{4}_{\gamma}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\gamma,\delta,\sigma_{1}}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{4}).

Deal with
J_{5}
term.

It is easy to check that

\displaystyle J_{5}=

\displaystyle\sum_{0<k\leq 6}C_{6}^{k}\!\!\int\partial_{y}\left\{\partial_{x}^{k}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{x}^{6-k}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\right\}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy+\!\!\int\partial_{y}\left\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\right\}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy

\displaystyle+\int\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\partial_{y}\{\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy-\sum_{0<k\leq 6}C_{6}^{k}\int\partial_{x}^{k}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\partial_{x}^{6-k}\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon\int\partial_{x}^{7}u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dxdy

\displaystyle\overset{def}{=}

\displaystyle J_{5,1}+J_{5,2}+J_{5,3}-J_{5,4}-J_{5,5}.

Integrating by part and using inequalities (
A.5
),
(
A.6
) and (
3.3
), we have

\displaystyle|J_{5,1}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\gamma}\|\partial_{x}(\frac{1}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}})\|_{H^{5}_{0}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}(\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-1}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\gamma,\kappa}(1+\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{6}_{0}}^{6})\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{5}_{\gamma}}(\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-1}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{3}\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{3},\kappa,\gamma,\delta,\sigma_{1}}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{19}).

Integrating by part and using lower bound condition (
3.1
), it holds

\displaystyle|J_{5,2}|\leq

\displaystyle C_{\kappa}\|\partial_{x}^{6}u^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-2}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}(\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-1}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\kappa,\delta}\|\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-1}\|_{L^{2}}(\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+2}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})

\displaystyle\times(\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}+\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-1}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\nu_{3}\|\partial_{y}w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\nu_{3},\kappa,\gamma,\delta,\sigma_{1}}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{5}).

Similarly, it is easy to obtain

\displaystyle|J_{5,3}|=

\displaystyle|\int\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\left(-\frac{\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}}{(\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty})^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{y}g_{w}^{\varepsilon}}{\varrho^{\varepsilon}+\varrho_{\infty}}\right)\cdot w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2\gamma}dx|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\delta,\kappa}(\|\partial_{y}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+2}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{y}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\sigma_{1}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})

\displaystyle\times\|\partial_{x}^{6}w^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma-2}\|_{L^{2}}\|w_{g}^{\varepsilon}\langle y\rangle^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\kappa,\gamma,\delta,\sigma_{1}}(1+\mathcal{E}_{1}(t)^{3}),

and

\displaystyle|J_{5,4}|\leq
